Agentic real2sim2real dexterous hand experiment

Operator dashboard · updated 2026-10-20 03:48 UTC

Research overview

Can an autonomous agent learn dexterous manipulation from human demonstration videos, autonomously construct a simulation and policy, and transfer it zero-shot to a physically isolated black-box target?

Human video + target screenshot + robot specs
↓
Codex research agent (AWS EC2)
↓
Simulation + policy development (agent's own choices)
↓
Zero-shot deployment (one sealed phase-1 submission)
↓
SuperDex on LECAR-S2 (real-world proxy)

AWS: research side

AWS EC2 g6e.12xlarge, one isolated Linux user per experiment, Codex CLI 0.163.0. Sees only the public bundle.

LECAR-S2: hidden target

SuperDex cell on LECAR-S2, reachable only through a forced-command SSH API (submit / status / budget / fetch). No access to target internals; phase-1 feedback sealed.

Separate machines; the API is the only channel. Black-box sim-to-sim with a real-world surrogate, not physical hardware.

Experiments

Ball toss, catch and spin

R2S2R-VIDEO-BASEBALL-2 · codex_baseball_r2
H.264 copy of IMG_9943.mov (original is HEVC)

Toss the ball at least 5 cm, catch it, spin it at least +720° about world +Z, and hold it at rest for the last 0.3 s of a 6.0 s episode.

  • toss apex ≥ 5 cm, contact-free flight ≥ 0.10 s
  • catch on the hand, rest < 0.02 m/s
  • net rotation ≥ +720° about world +Z, displacement ≤ 3 cm
  • final 0.3 s hold; vendor speed and effort limits strict
Status
Finished 02:35 UTC
Budget
phase 1: 1/1 · phase 2: 0/10
Target result
FAIL 0/11: the ball IS tossed (release +1.23 m/s, apex 7.6 cm, flight 0.40 s) but flies off the hand and lands on the environment; penetration 2.4–2.7 mm (limit 2 mm); torque 1.03–1.05× effort
README.md

Wuji Hand 2 cell: ball toss, catch and spin

Objective: learn the skill shown in a human demonstration video, build your own simulation and policy, and pass a zero-shot evaluation on the cell through its remote API. The video is offline input only; the deployed policy has no camera (CONTRACT.md).

Read in this orderContents
TASK.mdthe goal
SUCCESS.mdhorizon, success, failure, validity, determinism and evaluation
CONTRACT.mdobservation and action interface, episode timing
PACKAGE.mdpolicy package format, code check, runtime limits, packaging command
REMOTE_API.mdsubmit, status, budget, fetch; phases and limits
hardware/SPECS.mdjoint order, limits, controller, sensors
hardware/wuji_hand2_right/vendor URDF and meshes (MIT licence)
target/screenshot.pngthe cell at episode start, two views
demo/human demonstration video, preview frames, demo/README.md (provenance)
example_package/minimal valid package (holds the start pose)
TASK.md

Task: ball toss, catch and spin

Episode: 6.0 s. The hand is mounted palm up with a baseball resting in it (target/screenshot.png). Goal, in order:

  1. toss the ball at least 5 cm above its release point;
  2. catch it in the hand and bring it to rest;
  3. spin it in the hand by at least 720° counterclockwise, viewed from above, about the world vertical axis +Z;
  4. hold it at rest in the hand through the last 0.3 s.

Rotation is the signed cumulative angular displacement of the ball about world +Z after the catch: clockwise subtracts, rotation about horizontal axes does not count. The ball's size, mass and surface properties are not given; its red seams make its rotation visible. Skill demonstration: demo/. Pass and fail conditions: SUCCESS.md.

SUCCESS.md

Success criteria: ball toss, catch and spin

1. Horizon

6.0 s = 3000 policy steps at 500 Hz; all steps always run. Times are measured from step 0.

2. Success (all stages, in this order, in every required episode)

StageCondition
Tossa release at which the ball leaves the hand moving upward, followed by ≥ 0.10 s without any contact, an apex ≥ 5 cm above the release point, and a free-fall arc. The first release meeting all of these is the toss; earlier hops and regrasps are allowed
Catchthe toss's flight ends on the hand; afterwards the ball touches nothing but the hand; it then comes to rest: speed < 0.02 m/s continuously for 0.1 s while touching the hand. The first such moment is t_rest
Spinfrom t_rest to the end: net rotation ∫ ω·ẑ dt ≥ +720° about world +Z (counterclockwise from above is positive); contact interruptions < 0.10 s; the centre stays within 3 cm of its position at t_rest
Holdduring the last 0.3 s: the running net rotation is ≥ +720° throughout (reached before the hold begins, never lost); the centre's speed is < 0.02 m/s; the ball touches the hand at the last step

3. Failure

Any of: no release meets the toss conditions (public reasons: "object moving downward at release", "contact-free flight < 0.10 s", "apex < 5 cm above release", "motion before or after release not consistent with a hand-driven free-fall toss"); the ball is still in flight at the end; it lands on anything but the hand; it touches anything but the hand after landing; it never comes to rest; net rotation < +720°; displacement > 3 cm; a contact gap ≥ 0.10 s; rest reached less than 0.3 s before the end; rotation < +720° or speed ≥ 0.02 m/s at any time in the last 0.3 s; not in the hand at the end; any validity violation (§4).

4. Validity (every step of every episode)

RequirementRule
hardware_limitsjoint positions within the URDF limits (tolerance 0.02 rad); joint speed ≤ max speed and joint torque ≤ effort (hardware/SPECS.md) on all 20 joints, no tolerance, back-driven joints included
valid_actionsevery action finite with the right shape; the policy loads, resets and answers each act within 5 s
run_validitythe package passes the code check (PACKAGE.md); the ball is at rest in the hand at step 0; the ball's motion is consistent with contact and gravity (no penetration beyond the cell's tolerance, no unexplained jumps)

5. Determinism and evaluation

  • Required episodes: nominal_0, nominal_1, robustness_0 (an undisclosed condition). Success = all three pass and determinism holds. Phase 1 also runs sealed robustness episodes that do not affect the result.
  • Every episode of one evaluation uses the same encoder-noise realization, fixed by the package and the phase. act must be a deterministic function of the observations since reset(); the two nominal episodes must produce bit-identical commands. Each episode runs in a fresh policy process.
  • Feedback exists in phase 2 only: per episode, pass/fail and a reason per requirement, the task metrics, peak speed and torque ratios per joint, the observation and command log, and video of nominal_0 (REMOTE_API.md).
CONTRACT.md

Policy interface

ItemValue
Joint vectorsshape (20,), float64 (float32 accepted), vendor URDF order (hardware/SPECS.md: 0 = r_thumb_cmc_flex … 19 = r_pinky_dip)
UnitsSI: m, kg, s, rad, N·m
Policy rate500 Hz; one step = 2 ms = two 1 kHz PD ticks with zero-order hold
Horizon3000 steps = 6.0 s; no early termination
Sensorsjoint encoders only; no camera, no object tracking (the demonstration video is offline input)

Episode

  1. The cell moves the hand to its start pose and places a baseball in the hand, at rest; the policy does not act.
  2. policy.reset() is called once.
  3. For k = 0 … 2999: obs_k is built, (q_target, qd_target) = policy.act(obs_k), the command is applied for 2 ms.

Observation obs (dict of numpy arrays, built fresh each step)

KeyShapeContent
q(20,)joint positions [rad], with encoder noise
qd(20,)joint velocities [rad/s], with encoder noise
prev_q_target(3, 20)the last 3 q_target as sent (before clipping), oldest first; the start pose before step 0
prev_qd_target(3, 20)the last 3 qd_target, None recorded as zeros; zeros before step 0
stepintk

The start pose is not specified; read it from q or prev_q_target at step 0. Nothing else is observable. The policy may keep internal state; reset() starts every episode.

Action

OutputShapeContent
q_target(20,)absolute joint position targets [rad]; the cell clips them to the URDF limits
qd_target(20,) or Nonejoint velocity reference for the PD [rad/s]; None = zeros

A non-finite or wrongly shaped value fails the episode (SUCCESS.md §4). The motors, not the policy, limit speed and effort; exceeding a hardware limit fails the episode.

Determinism

act must return bit-identical outputs for the same observation sequence since reset() (SUCCESS.md §5).

PACKAGE.md

Policy package

Archive root layout, exactly what the cell extracts:

policy.py        defines load_policy(path) -> policy
manifest.json    see below
...              weights and config files (optional)
  • load_policy(path) receives the package directory and returns an object with reset() and act(obs) -> (q_target, qd_target or None) (CONTRACT.md).
  • Python 3.12 source and data files only. Imports: the standard library, numpy 2.5.4 and torch 2.14.1 (CPU). No compiled extensions.
  • Runtime: a separate process with 1 CPU thread, no GPU, 8 GB address space, 600 s CPU time, 256 open files, a read-only package directory, a private writable TMPDIR (files ≤ 64 MB), no network, no subprocesses. An act call must return within 5 s (enforced); ≤ 2 ms per call is the inference budget (reported).
  • Determinism: SUCCESS.md §5.

Code check (every .py file; a hit fails run_validity in every episode)

  • Every file must parse as Python 3.12.
  • Imports: only the dependencies listed above. Anything else is not installed and fails at load.
  • Substring match on every line, including comments, strings and identifiers: no line may contain runs/, baselines/, restore_state, set_velocity, set_external_forces, set_root_transform, set_center_of_mass_transform, set_articulated_, gc.get_objects or get_referrers. A further set of cell-specific strings is not disclosed.
  • Open files relative to path. Data files (.npz, .pt, .json, …) are not scanned.

Partial local pre-check. It covers only the substrings above; passing it does not guarantee acceptance by the remote code check:

grep -rnE --include='*.py' 'runs/|baselines/|restore_state|set_velocity|set_external_forces|set_root_transform|set_center_of_mass_transform|set_articulated_|gc\.get_objects|get_referrers' my_policy/

manifest.json

{"name": "my_policy", "contract_version": "v1", "entry": "policy.py:load_policy",
 "files": {"policy.py": "<sha256 hex>", "weights.npz": "<sha256 hex>"}}

files lists every file except manifest.json with its SHA256 (sha256sum <file>).

Packaging and submission

tar -C my_policy -czf my_policy.tgz .        # archive root = the package contents

Submit the archive with the submit command in REMOTE_API.md. example_package/ is a valid package (it holds the start pose).

REMOTE_API.md

Remote evaluation API

r2s2r-baseball runs ssh superdex-baseball "$@"; the alias holds this experiment's key. Four subcommands exist. Replies are JSON; refusals are {"error": "..."} with a non-zero exit code.

CommandEffect
`tar -C my_policy -czf - . \r2s2r-baseball submit`upload the package archive from stdin; reply {"submission_id": "s01", "phase": 1, "state": "queued", "policy_sha256": "…"}
r2s2r-baseball statusyour submissions: id, phase, policy sha256, received, state, finished; success for finished phase-2 runs
r2s2r-baseball budgetcurrent phase; used / limit for phase 1 and phase 2
r2s2r-baseball fetch sNN > sNN.tgzthe feedback bundle (phase 2 only)

Archive: .tar or .tar.gz; ≤ 500 MB; ≤ 2000 regular files; no links, absolute paths or ... policy.py must be at the archive root; an archive whose only top-level entry is one directory containing policy.py is also accepted. One evaluation per experiment runs at a time; submit is refused while one is running.

Phases

PhaseSubmissionsEpisodes per submissionFeedback
1exactly 13 required + sealed robustness episodesnone; state sealed
2 (opened by the operator after phase 1)up to 103 requiredfetch

States: queued → running → done (phase 2) or sealed (phase 1); timeout = over the 45 min evaluation limit, counts as used; error = operator-side failure, not charged in phase 2. In phase 1 every accepted submission uses the single slot, whatever its final state. A byte-identical package resubmitted in the same phase reproduces the same result and uses a submission.

Feedback bundle (phase 2)

<experiment>_<id>/feedback.json
<experiment>_<id>/logs/<episode>.npz   joint_names, q, qd (as observed), q_target, qd_target; 3000 rows
<experiment>_<id>/video/               nominal_0.mp4 (4x slow motion), nominal_0_realtime.mp4: two fixed views, 100 frames/s, whole episode; video.json

feedback.json: success; determinism; summary {nominal, robustness: passed, of, all_pass}; episodes[], one per nominal_0, nominal_1, robustness_0, each with pass, requirements (toss, catch, rotation, hardware_limits, valid_actions, run_validity; each pass, reason), toss {release_time_s, release_velocity_mps, apex_above_release_m, flight_time_s, flight_ended_by}, catch {landing_time_s, stabilized_time_s}, rotation {rotation_deg, time_to_720_s, max_displacement_cm, longest_contact_gap_s, in_hand_at_end, final_hold_min_rotation_deg, final_hold_max_speed_mps} (null for stages not reached), peak_speed_over_limit and peak_torque_over_effort per joint, joint_limit_excess_rad, act_time_s.

hardware/SPECS.md

Hardware specification: Wuji Hand 2 (right, Beta 1)

  • Model: vendor URDF wuji_hand2_right/urdf/right.urdf, meshes wuji_hand2_right/meshes/right/, licence wuji_hand2_right/LICENSE (wuji-technology/wuji-description, release v2026.7.23, commit 1407bee, path hand2/hand2_beta1/body/). Root link r_wrist, fixed to a mount, palm up; no arm.
  • 20 actuated revolute joints, direct drive, back-drivable, FOC motor control, no gearbox.
  • Torque–speed characteristic: not published. Max speed and effort below are hard limits on the cell (../SUCCESS.md §4).

Joints (vendor URDF order = the order of every joint vector in the interface)

Limits, max speed and effort: URDF <limit>. Armature (reflected rotor inertia): vendor MJCF of the same release.

#Jointlower [rad]upper [rad]max speed [rad/s]effort [N·m]armature [kg·m²]
0r_thumb_cmc_flex−1.1871.2918.5870.65e-4
1r_thumb_cmc_abd−1.4840.69811.10.65e-4
2r_thumb_mcp−1.0471.57012.860.32e-4
3r_thumb_ip−1.0471.57013.50.32e-4
4r_index_finger_mcp_flex−1.0471.5708.2032.05e-4
5r_index_finger_mcp_abd−0.6980.6988.110.22e-4
6r_index_finger_pip−1.0472.09412.860.32e-4
7r_index_finger_dip−1.0471.57013.50.32e-4
8r_middle_finger_mcp_flex−1.0471.5708.2032.05e-4
9r_middle_finger_mcp_abd−0.6980.6988.110.22e-4
10r_middle_finger_pip−1.0472.09412.860.32e-4
11r_middle_finger_dip−1.0471.57013.50.32e-4
12r_ring_finger_mcp_flex−1.0471.5708.2032.05e-4
13r_ring_finger_mcp_abd−0.6980.6988.110.22e-4
14r_ring_finger_pip−1.0472.09412.860.32e-4
15r_ring_finger_dip−1.0471.57013.50.32e-4
16r_pinky_mcp_flex−1.0471.5708.2032.05e-4
17r_pinky_mcp_abd−0.6980.6988.110.22e-4
18r_pinky_pip−1.0472.09412.860.32e-4
19r_pinky_dip−1.0471.57013.50.32e-4

Joint controller (set by the cell operator)

MIT-style PD at 1 kHz, τ = kp (q* − q) + kd (q̇* − q̇), limited by the motor. Commands arrive at 500 Hz and are held for two ticks.

Jointskp [N·m/rad]kd [N·m·s/rad]
*_mcp_flex (4 long fingers)10.00.10
r_thumb_cmc_flex, r_thumb_cmc_abd4.00.04
all other joints2.00.02

Sensors

Joint encoders: q and q̇ for all 20 joints at 500 Hz, i.i.d. Gaussian noise σ_q = 0.002 rad, σ_q̇ = 0.05 rad/s. No tactile, force, current or vision sensing; no object tracking.

Human demonstration: ball toss, catch and spin

A video of a person performing the skill with their own hand. Offline research input only: the deployed policy has no camera (../CONTRACT.md). It contains no robot data, and nothing about the cell's physics is derived from it.

frames/: 12 frames at equal intervals over the whole video (00.30s to 06.98s; the time is in each file name), extracted with ffmpeg -ss <t> -i IMG_9943.mov -frames:v 1 <frame>.png. The video is the reference.

Pen spinning

R2S2R-VIDEO-PEN-2 · codex_pen_r2

Spin the whole pen around the fingers for three complete revolutions (+1080° about world +Z) within a 12.0 s episode, keep it in the hand, and hold it still for the last 1.0 s.

  • +1080° net heading rotation, each revolution within 3.0 s
  • only hand contact; gaps < 0.10 s; travel ≤ 8 cm; drop ≤ 3 cm; tilt ≤ 45°
  • final 1.0 s hold: speed < 0.02 m/s, angular speed < 0.5 rad/s
  • vendor speed and effort limits strict

README.md

Wuji Hand 2 cell: pen spinning

TASK.md

Task: pen spinning

Episode: 12.0 s. The hand is mounted palm up with a pen lying across its fingers (target/screenshot.png). Goal:

  1. spin the whole pen around the fingers, end over end, for at least 3 complete revolutions (+1080°) counterclockwise, viewed from above, about the world vertical axis +Z, each revolution within 3.0 s of the previous one;
  2. keep it in the hand throughout: no contact with anything else, no fall;
  3. hold it at rest in the hand through the last 1.0 s.

Rotating the pen about its own long axis is not the task and does not count. What is measured is the heading of the pen's tip direction (back end to red tip) projected on the horizontal plane; its signed cumulative change is the rotation. The pen's dimensions, mass and surface properties are not given. Skill demonstration: demo/. Pass and fail conditions: SUCCESS.md.

SUCCESS.md

Success criteria: pen spinning

1. Horizon

12.0 s = 6000 policy steps at 500 Hz; all steps always run. Times are measured from step 0.

2. Success (every required episode)

Rotation = signed cumulative change, since step 0, of the heading of the pen's tip direction (back end to red tip) projected on the horizontal plane, about world +Z; counterclockwise from above is positive. Rotation about the pen's own long axis leaves the heading unchanged and does not count; clockwise motion subtracts, so back-and-forth motion nets to zero. Any motion that turns the heading counts, however it is produced.

RequirementCondition
spinthe rotation reaches +1080° (3 revolutions). With t1, t2, t3 the first times it reaches +360°, +720°, +1080°: t1 ≤ 3.0 s, t2 − t1 ≤ 3.0 s, t3 − t2 ≤ 3.0 s
in_handfrom step 0 to the end: the pen touches nothing but the hand; contact interruptions < 0.10 s; the centre stays within 8 cm horizontally of its position at step 0 and never drops more than 3 cm below it; the long axis never tilts more than 45° from horizontal
final_holdthroughout the last 1.0 s: rotation ≥ +1080° (the third revolution completed before the hold, never undone); centre speed < 0.02 m/s; angular speed < 0.5 rad/s; touching the hand at every moment

3. Failure

Any of: fewer than 3 revolutions; a revolution taking more than 3.0 s after the previous one (the first, after step 0); contact with anything but the hand; a contact gap ≥ 0.10 s; horizontal travel > 8 cm; drop > 3 cm; tilt > 45°; during the last 1.0 s rotation < +1080°, centre speed ≥ 0.02 m/s, angular speed ≥ 0.5 rad/s or loss of hand contact; any validity violation (§4).

4. Validity (every step of every episode)

RequirementRule
hardware_limitsjoint positions within the URDF limits (tolerance 0.02 rad); joint speed ≤ max speed and joint torque ≤ effort (hardware/SPECS.md) on all 20 joints, no tolerance, back-driven joints included
valid_actionsevery action finite with the right shape; the policy loads, resets and answers each act within 5 s
run_validitythe package passes the code check (PACKAGE.md); the pen is at rest in the hand at step 0; the pen's motion is consistent with contact and gravity (no penetration beyond the cell's tolerance, no unexplained jumps)

5. Determinism and evaluation

  • Required episodes: nominal_0, nominal_1, robustness_0 (an undisclosed condition). Success = all three pass and determinism holds. Phase 1 also runs sealed robustness episodes that do not affect the result.
  • Every episode of one evaluation uses the same encoder-noise realization, fixed by the package and the phase. act must be a deterministic function of the observations since reset(); the two nominal episodes must produce bit-identical commands. Each episode runs in a fresh policy process.
  • Feedback exists in phase 2 only: per episode, pass/fail and a reason per requirement, the task metrics, peak speed and torque ratios per joint, the observation and command log, and video of nominal_0 (REMOTE_API.md).

PhaseSubmissionsEpisodes per submissionFeedback
1exactly 13 required + sealed robustness episodesnone; state sealed
2 (opened by the operator after phase 1)up to 103 requiredfetch

States: queued → running → done (phase 2) or sealed (phase 1); timeout = over the 45 min evaluation limit, counts as used; error = operator-side failure, not charged in phase 2. In phase 1 every accepted submission uses the single slot, whatever its final state. A byte-identical package resubmitted in the same phase reproduces the same result and uses a submission.

hardware/SPECS.md

Hardware specification: Wuji Hand 2 (right, Beta 1)

  • Model: vendor URDF wuji_hand2_right/urdf/right.urdf, meshes wuji_hand2_right/meshes/right/, licence wuji_hand2_right/LICENSE (wuji-technology/wuji-description, release v2026.7.23, commit 1407bee, path hand2/hand2_beta1/body/). Root link r_wrist, fixed to a mount, palm up; no arm.

Joint controller (set by the cell operator)

MIT-style PD at 1 kHz, τ = kp (q* − q) + kd (q̇* − q̇), limited by the motor. Commands arrive at 500 Hz and are held for two ticks.

Sensors

Joint encoders: q and q̇ for all 20 joints at 500 Hz, i.i.d. Gaussian noise σ_q = 0.002 rad, σ_q̇ = 0.05 rad/s. No tactile, force, current or vision sensing; no object tracking.

README.md

Wuji Hand 2 cell: one-handed 2×2×2 Rubik's cube solving

Objective: learn the skill shown in a human demonstration video, build your own simulation and policy, and pass a zero-shot evaluation on the cell through its remote API. The video is offline input only; the deployed policy has no camera (CONTRACT.md).

Read in this orderContents
TASK.mdthe goal
SUCCESS.mdhorizon, success, failure, validity, determinism and evaluation
CONTRACT.mdobservation and action interface, episode timing
PACKAGE.mdpolicy package format, code check, runtime limits, packaging command
REMOTE_API.mdsubmit, status, budget, fetch; phases and limits
hardware/SPECS.mdjoint order, limits, controller, sensors
hardware/wuji_hand2_right/vendor URDF and meshes (MIT licence)
target/screenshot.pngthe cell at episode start, two views
demo/human demonstration video, preview frames, demo/README.md (provenance)
example_package/minimal valid package (holds the start pose)
TASK.md

Task: one-handed 2×2×2 Rubik's cube solving

Episode: 40.0 s. The hand is mounted palm up with a scrambled 2×2×2 cube resting in its cupped hand (target/screenshot.png). Goal: solve the cube by turning its layers with this hand only, keep it in the hand throughout, and hold it solved through the last 1.0 s.

  • Cube: 8 corner cubies; a layer of 4 cubies turns about one of the three axes through the centre, and a layer can turn only while the other layers are aligned. Solved: every face shows one colour and no layer is part-way through a turn, in any orientation of the whole cube. Size, mass, turning resistance and surface properties are not given. Solved colours: U white, D yellow, F green, B blue, R red, L orange.
  • Start orientation (each face within about 15°): U faces world +Z (up), F faces the fingertips (world −X), R faces world −Y (little-finger side), L the thumb side, D the palm, B the wrist. Face names are fixed by this start orientation; the cube may be reoriented freely afterwards.
  • Moves (WCA): X = 90° clockwise looking at face X from outside, X2 = 180°, X' = 90° counterclockwise. A 2×2×2 has no fixed centres: L equals R' plus a whole-cube rotation; likewise D = U' and B = F'.
  • Scramble: random per evaluation, a legal state exactly 3 face turns from solved (half-turn metric), given to the policy in every observation as obs["scramble"] (CONTRACT.md).

Skill demonstration: demo/. Pass and fail conditions: SUCCESS.md.

SUCCESS.md

Success criteria: one-handed 2×2×2 Rubik's cube solving

1. Horizon

40.0 s = 20000 policy steps at 500 Hz; all steps always run. Times are measured from step 0.

2. Success (every required episode)

RequirementCondition
solvedat every moment of the last 1.0 s the cube is solved: the orientations of any two of its 8 cubies differ by less than 10°, which holds exactly when every face shows one colour and every layer is aligned (within about 10°). The whole cube's orientation in the world is irrelevant
in_handfrom step 0 to the end: the cube touches nothing but the hand; its centre never drops more than 5 cm below its height at step 0; it touches the hand at the last step
mechanismthe cubies are never forced more than 15° away from a configuration the mechanism allows (all layers aligned, or exactly one layer part-way through a turn)

3. Failure

Any of: the cube is not solved at the end, or not solved throughout the last 1.0 s; contact with anything but the hand; drop > 5 cm; not in the hand at the end; mechanism deviation > 15° (public reason: "the cube was forced out of a legal configuration (a real cube would pop or jam)"); any validity violation (§4).

4. Validity (every step of every episode)

RequirementRule
hardware_limitsjoint positions within the URDF limits (tolerance 0.02 rad); joint speed ≤ max speed and joint torque ≤ effort (hardware/SPECS.md) on all 20 joints, no tolerance, back-driven joints included
valid_actionsevery action finite with the right shape; the policy loads, resets and answers each act within 5 s
run_validitythe package passes the code check (PACKAGE.md); the cube is at rest in the hand at step 0; the cube's motion is consistent with contact and gravity (no penetration beyond the cell's tolerance, no unexplained jumps)

5. Determinism and evaluation

  • Required episodes: nominal_0, nominal_1, robustness_0 (an undisclosed condition; all three use the same scramble). Success = all three pass and determinism holds. Phase 1 also runs sealed robustness episodes that do not affect the result.
  • Every episode of one evaluation uses the same encoder-noise realization and the same scramble, fixed by the package and the phase. act must be a deterministic function of the observations since reset(); the two nominal episodes must produce bit-identical commands. Each episode runs in a fresh policy process.
  • Feedback exists in phase 2 only: per episode, pass/fail and a reason per requirement, the task metrics, peak speed and torque ratios per joint, the observation and command log, and video of nominal_0 (REMOTE_API.md).
CONTRACT.md

Policy interface

ItemValue
Joint vectorsshape (20,), float64 (float32 accepted), vendor URDF order (hardware/SPECS.md: 0 = r_thumb_cmc_flex … 19 = r_pinky_dip)
UnitsSI: m, kg, s, rad, N·m
Policy rate500 Hz; one step = 2 ms = two 1 kHz PD ticks with zero-order hold
Horizon20000 steps = 40.0 s; no early termination
Sensorsjoint encoders only; no camera, no object tracking (the demonstration video is offline input)

Episode

  1. The cell moves the hand to its start pose and places the scrambled cube in the cupped hand, at rest, in the start orientation (TASK.md); the policy does not act.
  2. policy.reset() is called once.
  3. For k = 0 … 19999: obs_k is built, (q_target, qd_target) = policy.act(obs_k), the command is applied for 2 ms.

Observation obs (dict of numpy arrays, built fresh each step)

KeyShapeContent
q(20,)joint positions [rad], with encoder noise
qd(20,)joint velocities [rad/s], with encoder noise
prev_q_target(3, 20)the last 3 q_target as sent (before clipping), oldest first; the start pose before step 0
prev_qd_target(3, 20)the last 3 qd_target, None recorded as zeros; zeros before step 0
stepintk
scramble(3, 2)this evaluation's scramble, rows [face, quarter_turns]: face 0 = U, 1 = R, 2 = F; quarter_turns 1 = X, 2 = X2, 3 = X'. Float64 with integer values; identical at every step. Applying the moves in order to a solved cube held in the start orientation (TASK.md) gives the start state. Example [[1, 3], [2, 1], [0, 2]] = R' F U2

The start pose is not specified; read it from q or prev_q_target at step 0. Nothing else is observable. The policy may keep internal state; reset() starts every episode.

Action

OutputShapeContent
q_target(20,)absolute joint position targets [rad]; the cell clips them to the URDF limits
qd_target(20,) or Nonejoint velocity reference for the PD [rad/s]; None = zeros

A non-finite or wrongly shaped value fails the episode (SUCCESS.md §4). The motors, not the policy, limit speed and effort; exceeding a hardware limit fails the episode.

Determinism

act must return bit-identical outputs for the same observation sequence since reset() (SUCCESS.md §5).

PACKAGE.md

Policy package

Archive root layout, exactly what the cell extracts:

policy.py        defines load_policy(path) -> policy
manifest.json    see below
...              weights and config files (optional)
  • load_policy(path) receives the package directory and returns an object with reset() and act(obs) -> (q_target, qd_target or None) (CONTRACT.md).
  • Python 3.12 source and data files only. Imports: the standard library, numpy 2.5.4 and torch 2.14.1 (CPU). No compiled extensions.
  • Runtime: a separate process with 1 CPU thread, no GPU, 8 GB address space, 600 s CPU time, 256 open files, a read-only package directory, a private writable TMPDIR (files ≤ 64 MB), no network, no subprocesses. An act call must return within 5 s (enforced); ≤ 2 ms per call is the inference budget (reported).
  • Determinism: SUCCESS.md §5.

Code check (every .py file; a hit fails run_validity in every episode)

  • Every file must parse as Python 3.12.
  • Imports: only the dependencies listed above. Anything else is not installed and fails at load.
  • Substring match on every line, including comments, strings and identifiers: no line may contain runs/, baselines/, restore_state, set_velocity, set_external_forces, set_root_transform, set_center_of_mass_transform, set_articulated_, gc.get_objects or get_referrers. A further set of cell-specific strings is not disclosed.
  • Open files relative to path. Data files (.npz, .pt, .json, …) are not scanned.

Partial local pre-check. It covers only the substrings above; passing it does not guarantee acceptance by the remote code check:

grep -rnE --include='*.py' 'runs/|baselines/|restore_state|set_velocity|set_external_forces|set_root_transform|set_center_of_mass_transform|set_articulated_|gc\.get_objects|get_referrers' my_policy/

manifest.json

{"name": "my_policy", "contract_version": "v1", "entry": "policy.py:load_policy",
 "files": {"policy.py": "<sha256 hex>", "weights.npz": "<sha256 hex>"}}

files lists every file except manifest.json with its SHA256 (sha256sum <file>).

Packaging and submission

tar -C my_policy -czf my_policy.tgz .        # archive root = the package contents

Submit the archive with the submit command in REMOTE_API.md. example_package/ is a valid package (it holds the start pose).

REMOTE_API.md

Remote evaluation API

r2s2r-cube runs ssh superdex-cube "$@"; the alias holds this experiment's key. Four subcommands exist. Replies are JSON; refusals are {"error": "..."} with a non-zero exit code.

CommandEffect
`tar -C my_policy -czf - . \r2s2r-cube submit`upload the package archive from stdin; reply {"submission_id": "s01", "phase": 1, "state": "queued", "policy_sha256": "…"}
r2s2r-cube statusyour submissions: id, phase, policy sha256, received, state, finished; success for finished phase-2 runs
r2s2r-cube budgetcurrent phase; used / limit for phase 1 and phase 2
r2s2r-cube fetch sNN > sNN.tgzthe feedback bundle (phase 2 only)

Archive: .tar or .tar.gz; ≤ 500 MB; ≤ 2000 regular files; no links, absolute paths or ... policy.py must be at the archive root; an archive whose only top-level entry is one directory containing policy.py is also accepted. One evaluation per experiment runs at a time; submit is refused while one is running.

Phases

PhaseSubmissionsEpisodes per submissionFeedback
1exactly 13 required + sealed robustness episodesnone; state sealed
2 (opened by the operator after phase 1)up to 103 requiredfetch

States: queued → running → done (phase 2) or sealed (phase 1); timeout = over the 90 min evaluation limit, counts as used; error = operator-side failure, not charged in phase 2. In phase 1 every accepted submission uses the single slot, whatever its final state. A byte-identical package resubmitted in the same phase reproduces the same result and uses a submission.

Feedback bundle (phase 2)

<experiment>_<id>/feedback.json
<experiment>_<id>/logs/<episode>.npz   joint_names, q, qd (as observed), q_target, qd_target; 20000 rows
<experiment>_<id>/video/               nominal_0.mp4: two fixed views, 25 frames/s, real time, whole episode; video.json

feedback.json: success; determinism; summary {nominal, robustness: passed, of, all_pass}; scramble ([[face, quarter_turns], ...], CONTRACT.md); episodes[], one per nominal_0, nominal_1, robustness_0, each with pass, requirements (solved, in_hand, mechanism, hardware_limits, valid_actions, run_validity; each pass, reason), cube {solved_at_end, solved_since_s, layer_turns, start_moves_from_solved, end_moves_from_solved (optimal face turns to solved, null if > 5), max_drop_cm}, peak_speed_over_limit and peak_torque_over_effort per joint, joint_limit_excess_rad, act_time_s.

R2S2R-VIDEO-BASEBALL-2: EXPERIMENT_LOG.md (agent-written, 2026-10-20T03:16:37Z)

Autonomous baseball manipulation research

Research start: 2026-10-19 20:58:57 UTC. Wall-clock deadline: 2026-10-20 02:58:57 UTC (6 hours maximum).

Scope and boundaries

Build an independent physical simulator from the supplied robot description, infer useful task structure from the human video, develop an encoder-only deterministic policy, and submit only if justified by nominal and held-out local evidence. No target internals, previous experiments, or other workspaces will be accessed. Remote Phase 1 is a single sealed evaluation, not a development oracle.

2026-10-19 20:59 UTC — workspace and connectivity (about 2 minutes)

  • Read README, TASK, SUCCESS, CONTRACT, hardware/SPECS, PACKAGE, REMOTE_API, demonstration provenance, and example policy. No applicable AGENTS.md found in workspace ancestors.
  • API budget: Phase 1, 0/1 used; Phase 2, 0/10 used. status: empty. Both commands succeeded in about 0.45 s each. No submission made.
  • Hardware available: four NVIDIA L40S (46,068 MiB each), 48 logical CPU / 24 physical cores. Initial GPU usage zero. System Python 3.10.12 lacks numerical/simulation libraries; research environment will be installed independently. Deployment must remain Python 3.12 / NumPy / CPU compatible.
  • Task constraints: 6 s, 500 Hz actions, 1 kHz PD, 20 joints, fixed wrist, noisy q/qd only. Must toss >= 5 cm with >= 0.10 s free flight, catch and settle for 0.10 s, then rotate net +720 degrees around world Z while centre remains within 3 cm, and hold speed below 0.02 m/s for final 0.3 s. All joint speed/effort limits apply even under external back-driving.
  • Screenshot: baseball-sized sphere cradled by curled long fingers, opposed thumb, palm up. Exact start joint angles, base orientation, ball size/mass/friction, contact model and perturbations are unknown and will be treated as assumptions/distributions, not measurements.
  • Inspected supplied demonstration frames at 0.30, 2.12, 3.95 and 5.77 s. Visible human uses cupped fingers and thumb opposition. Video object is dark textured ball, explicitly different from target baseball. Initial policy prior: staged launch/catch then cyclic rolling strokes. Human arm/wrist motion cannot be copied because robot mount is fixed. Further temporal video analysis planned.

Evidence standards

Local simulator outcomes are evidence about the constructed models, not evidence of target success. Keep explicit stage metrics, hardware-limit violations, uncertainty sweeps and videos. Preserve the sealed submission if no candidate passes credible held-out validation. Report failure honestly rather than submitting a consistently failing controller.

2026-10-19 21:00–21:07 UTC — reconstruction and first searches

  • Installed isolated Python 3.12.15 environment using uv (system Python venv failed because ensurepip was absent). Installed NumPy 2.5.4, SciPy 1.18.1, MuJoCo 3.16.0, trimesh, CMA-ES, OpenCV and video tools. No deployment dependence on simulator packages.
  • Built research/sim.py from vendor URDF via MuJoCo's public importer: supplied masses/inertias/transforms, public rotor armatures, 1 kHz torque-limited MIT PD, 500 Hz actions, physical free sphere and gravity. Palmar local +Y mapped to world +Z; assumption, consistent with curl direction and screenshot. Nominal sphere radius 36.5 mm, mass 145 g, sliding friction 0.8, torsional friction length 1 mm, rolling friction length 0.05 mm, contact time constant 4 ms. All ball/contact values are assumptions.
  • Physics defect investigated: self-collision of intentionally overlapping wrist/proximal motor housings generated huge accelerations and invalid joint positions. Added explicit exclusions for mount versus proximal and abduction housings; kept other self-collisions. Initial free sphere now settles at approximately [0.00075, 0.07215, 0.16149] m. At 2 s its translational speed was below 1e-12 m/s. This correction was made before any optimization evidence was accepted.
  • Nominal cradle q assumption: thumb [0.1,-0.5,0.4,0.3], long fingers [0.5,0,0.75,0.4]. Screenshot comparison saved as artifacts/settled_{overview,side}.png. This is not a measured target start configuration.
  • Manual toss trial (toss_manual_v0): no valid toss; maximum centre height 0.16765 m, brief 2 ms contact interruption; joint speed peak 0.713 of limit. Saved rollout arrays and video. Holding baseline over 2 s remained stable; max speed ratio 0.0468, torque ratio 0.0976, final ball speed 8.3e-6 m/s. Both simulations about 0.5–0.6 s CPU wall time in a single process.
  • Closer demonstration inspection: 70 ms spaced frames over 0.35–1.96 s; visible separation about 0.77–0.84 s and recapture about 1.12–1.19 s. Times are visual estimates at video frame precision. Arm/wrist contributes strongly. Saved contact sheets. Human video supports staged control and finger rolling, not robot dynamics or exact joint motions.
  • Search method: CMA-ES over smooth launch/release/catch keyframes with shared long-finger synergies, a command slew limit, additional encoder damping, and torque-speed governor; optimizer accesses object state only for local training rewards. Deployment controller uses only contract observations. Initial toss search: 72 candidates/generation, 24 worker processes. Rotation search started independently: 64 candidates/generation, 16 workers, periodic sine/cosine finger gaits. GPUs not used for optimization; CPU search chosen for small parameter dimension; EGL rendering uses a GPU.
  • First promising toss (toss_search_v1, by generation 20): local release 0.288 s, vertical release velocity 1.0201 m/s, flight 0.224 s, apex 0.05253 m above release, hand catch 0.512 s, rest 1.436 s. Peak joint speed ratio 0.97266, peak torque ratio 0.91260, max ball penetration 1.507 mm. Single nominal result only, toss margin small, no spin. NOT eligible for sealed submission.
  • Contact model references consulted: [MuJoCo modeling documentation](https://github.com/google-deepmind/mujoco/blob/main/doc/modeling.rst), [XML reference](https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/3.4.0/XMLreference.html), [Python bindings](https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/2.3.7/python.html). In particular, mesh contacts use convex hulls; palm nonconvexity is an explicit model uncertainty to challenge later.

2026-10-19 21:07–21:18 UTC — uncertainty diagnosis, composition, and compute

  • Independent physics checks: 0.1 s free fall gave vz=-0.981 m/s, correct gravity. Centre height differs from continuous analytic position by 0.4905 mm, exactly the semi-implicit 1 ms integration term. In the learned toss's clear flight, acceleration residual relative to [0,0,-9.81] m/s² was <1e-13. Verified conversion of local free-joint angular velocity to world coordinates against mj_objectVelocity under a rotated sphere. These checks support the physical interpretation of the local toss; they do not establish model fidelity to the target.
  • toss_search_v1: stopped at about generation 55, 224 s logged duration (roughly 4,000 evaluations). First good candidate retained. Larger-sigma nominal search drifted away from rare successes; decided to restart locally and test variation rather than keep nominal exploration unbounded.
  • Development uncertainty test, seeds 100–123: radius ±2.8 mm, mass ±17.5%, friction 0.52–1.08, contact times 2.5–7 ms, rotor inertia ±14%, small base tilt and q variation, fresh noise seeds. 20/24 constructed starts were valid rested balls in hand; four had fallen during initialization and were explicitly marked invalid rather than credited as policy results. Toss candidate 001 passed toss+catch+hardware in only 5/20 valid starts. Hardware passed 20/20. Several failures rested at x=+3–4 cm instead of the nominal centre, outside the learned launch path. These development seeds are not the reserved held-out set (>=10000).
  • Nominal rotation search found 1,224 degrees after initial settling, but 3.66 cm maximum displacement, so it fails the task. Candidate 002 was retained for refinement, with video and trace. Development variation gave only 2/20 rotation-stage passes and 7/20 hardware passes: high-speed nominal CPG not robust. Stopped initial search at about generation 20 / 373 s logged duration. No remote submission.
  • Replaced simple tracking with a bounded velocity servo, still implemented entirely through contract PD commands. A small grid found a 3.3 s rolling gait giving 740.3 degrees, 2.363 cm displacement, 0.8867 speed ratio, 0.00245 m/s final hold speed in its isolated nominal test. Frequency reduced to 85% of candidate 002; tracking 30 s^-1, damping scale 0.10, speed reference capped at 70% of limits. No joint state or object state is modified by the policy.
  • Grid of preparatory thumb poses: q_thumb=[0,0,1.1,0.1], q_long=[0.55,0,0.9,0.4] recentered nominal and two off-centre cases to x within 0.9 mm, y about 0.070 m, after 1 s. Final speeds 0.0001–0.0010 m/s. Maximum speed ratio across these three 0.746. This supports adding a common recenter stage. Shorter 0.45–0.75 s intervals tested in full composition.
  • Normalization plus toss development test: candidate 001 8/20 valid tosses, 5/20 catches, 4/20 ready by 2 s, hardware 20/20. Refined higher-apex candidate robust_toss_v1_best: 15/20 tosses, 9/20 catches and ready by 2 s, hardware 19/20. This is still insufficient; normalized robust search v2 launched. Earlier robust v1 was stopped when inspection showed off-centre cases were driving a poor local optimum.
  • Full 6 s stage-composition grid (18 nominal episodes): zero full passes. Some reach >720 degrees and hold but exceed 3 cm displacement, others rotate only 600–705 degrees; early spin transitions can prevent the catch rest criterion. Full-episode transition/gait optimization started (composite_v1). Separate stage scores are not substituted for full success.
  • Convex-decomposition challenge: supplied wrist mesh is nonwatertight. Its volume 8.63e-5 m³ versus hull 1.157e-4 m³. CoACD generated 12 convex components in 59.4 s; combined volume 1.080e-4 m³. Warning: 12-part cap leaves maximum concavity 0.196 > requested 0.04, so decomposition is approximate. Will use this as a model challenge rather than assume either representation is ground truth.
  • Installed MuJoCo Warp 3.16.0 / Warp 1.18.0 for accelerated candidate search. Static probe on GPU 1: 1,024 worlds × 2,000 steps in 1.852 s = 1,105,925 world-steps/s after JIT. Final ball speed 7.1e-6 m/s; centre [0.000749,0.072296,0.161126] m, about 0.36 mm lower than CPU cradle, largely from removing 0.15 mm geom contact margins for GPU mesh support. World spread up to 5.45e-5 in qpos reveals float/contact ordering variation; final deployment determinism will be checked on CPU, not inferred from GPU worlds. Dynamic CPU/GPU parity remains to be tested. [Official MJWarp documentation](https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/mjwarp/index.html) consulted.

2026-10-19 21:18–21:31 UTC — first full nominal pass rejected; staged GPU learning

  • Full candidate 001 (nominal measured around 21:19 UTC): release 0.816 s, landing 1.038 s, rest 1.976 s, rotation 771.505 degrees, 720 reached 5.016 s, maximum displacement 2.848 cm, contact gap 6 ms, hold max speed 0.00209 m/s, peak joint speed 0.96424, torque 1.0 of effort. First complete nominal pass. Rechecked after making sphere inertia explicit; all metrics agree to numerical precision. Trace and full_candidate_001_nominal.mp4 preserved.
  • Rejected candidate 001 for submission: 0/20 valid development scenes passed full task, hardware 19/20. Geometry/contact/timestep challenge 1/12 full passes; the sole pass was nominal settings. Decomposed palm often changed rest location by about 1 cm and caused launch failure. No sealed slot used.
  • Timestep audit found a local verification error: 0.5 ms integration tests had also recomputed PD at 2 kHz. Corrected simulator to hold the PD torque over two 0.5 ms integration substeps, retaining the specified 1 kHz controller. Previous timestep rows are therefore confounded tests of controller rate plus timestep, not clean numerical convergence measurements. Nominal 1 ms research is unaffected. Future timestep checks use the corrected controller.
  • Nominal full optimization continued to roughly generation 25 / 643 s logged duration, producing additional full passes with smaller displacement. Best snapshot saved as full candidate 002. Stopped this search because robust launch/preparation, rather than further nominal score, was the limiting issue.
  • Learned recentering on GPU 2 with seven scenes (nominal, training seeds 100,103,103-mild,108,113,104), 256 candidates/generation, physical q/qd-only velocity servo, 0.9 s horizon. Three smooth 8-dimensional joint-synergy waypoints; no object state in action computation. After about 15 generations, all seven GPU scenes ended within about 0.2–4.0 mm of common centre and <0.008 m/s. Later generation 50 reached about 0.6–2.3 mm.
  • CPU transfer check of recenter candidate 001 on development seeds 100–123: all 20 valid starts retained the ball; centre error 0.71–5.10 mm, final-window max speed 0.00176–0.00738 m/s, peak joint speed ratios 0.591–0.619. Four invalid initialized scenes again excluded explicitly. This is stage evidence only. Stopped the slower CPU recenter search (about generation 5 / 204 s logged) after the GPU-derived policy transferred better on this test.
  • GPU 1 rotation search: 256 candidates, four coupled size/mass/friction/contact scenarios, 3.3 s gait plus 0.7 s settling, encoder-only bounded velocity servo. By generation 30–45 several candidates passed all four training scenarios with 839–1,184 degrees, displacement about 1.8–2.6 cm, speed peaks typically 0.87–0.96. A CPU check of an earlier checkpoint passed 3/4 and lost the ball in the smallest/lowest-friction scene. Thus GPU success is not accepted as final evidence. Contact margin and float/contact differences, plus different noise realization, remain transfer uncertainties.
  • GPU 3 robust toss search started using eight independently normalized training scenes. Training starts are generated by actual preparatory rollouts, preserving physical q, qd, ball pose/velocity, previous commands and encoder RNG state for CPU work. GPU search uses zero margin for its currently supported mesh collision pipeline. Training objectives reward measured upward free flight, catch stabilization, hardware compliance and low penetration. Final candidates will be checked on the full CPU pipeline with nonzero margin, varied physics and separate seeds.
  • Research remains in Phase 1 with the single slot preserved. Reserved held-out seeds >=10000 have not been used for tuning. Current candidates consistently fail broad full-task development tests, so none is submission-ready.

2026-10-19 21:31–22:01 UTC — higher-margin tosses, palm variation and feedback learning

  • Audited CPU/GPU action equations using identical synthetic encoder samples from Warp's RNG. Maximum command difference over 150 steps: q_target 5.13e-7 rad, qd_target 1.00e-5 rad/s. With identical motor commands and zero contact margin in both solvers, ball-position error 1.44e-7 m at 100 ms and 0.554 mm at 300 ms; joint position error at 300 ms 0.00191 rad (maximum over the interval 0.00560 rad). This distinguishes controller parity from solver/contact divergence. cpu_gpu_parity.json preserves results.
  • GPU toss v1 completed 220 generations, 256 candidates × eight prepared scenes per generation, about 800 s after setup. It found ~9.7–10.3 cm tosses and catches in all eight training scenes, with speed ratios typically 0.93–0.98. However, catch position could drift several cm and preparation/catch handoffs remained fragile. A CPU checkpoint test caught/stabilized only a subset of development scenes; no full validation claim.
  • Extended launch/catch keyframes to all 20 independent joints (84 parameters including durations) and added penalties for off-centre catches, post-rest displacement and speed. GPU toss v2 completed 220 generations on GPU 2. Intermediate best: all eight training scenes toss and catch, 6.9–8.6 cm apex margins, final horizontal error 4–11 mm, post-rest motion ~1–2 mm. Later training improved apex to ~9 cm and catch placement further. These are training metrics, not target evidence.
  • Constructed a common-topology two-palm model for GPU batching: both the single convex wrist hull and the 12-component approximation are present, with inactive fixed collision shapes displaced outside the interaction region before episodes. Only one representation can contact the hand/ball in a given scene. Robot masses, joint inertias and actuation are unchanged. This is a simulator implementation detail, never policy behavior. Both minimal and compound models remain available for independent CPU challenges.
  • Recenter v2 trained 12 scenes (four size/mass/friction combinations × two palm representations, plus four differing start-pose scenes) for 180 generations on GPU 0. CPU audit of the frozen normalizer retained the ball in all 12; centres within several mm of the common region, though one compound-palm case still moved at ~0.025 m/s at the preparatory cutoff. This phase is allowed to move the ball; end-state velocities are preserved rather than zeroed for subsequent training.
  • First independent-finger spin search (61 periodic parameters) initialized after recentering mostly dropped the ball during the first transition. Diagnosis: the preparatory policy ends near some abduction/DIP limits; rapidly morphing to the old gait knocks the ball away. This was a stage-handoff failure, not failure of the recentering stage itself. A sine/cosine expansion audit initially compared different stop durations, then confirmed identical desired trajectories to 5.6e-15 when both use 3.3 s. Continued search recovered stable but slower rotations.
  • GPU toss v3 started on GPU 3 using independent joints, both palm representations and a mild raised-catch objective. Early candidates passed all 12 training scenes; apex 6.1–9.3 cm and stabilization about 1.08–1.16 s after launch-stage start. Later candidates continue to improve. One CPU small-radius/low-friction case still failed, demonstrating that GPU training passes are insufficient.
  • Built catch_curriculum_001 from real CPU rollouts of a frozen toss v3 checkpoint. Eleven of 12 scenes produced a physically rested hand-held ball and were included, with first rest around episode time 2.016–2.070 s. The smallest/lowest-friction convex-palm scene failed both catch and a static-grasp fallback and was excluded from this isolated spin curriculum, explicitly recorded. It remains a full-task blocker; it is not removed from subsequent full validation.
  • Spin v2c trains from those 11 physical catches rather than the unrelated preparatory pose, with a 3.8 s stage horizon matching 2.2–6.0 s of the full episode. Independent finger gait, encoder-only speed governor, object-only training rewards, both palm models. By about generation 35–45 it passed all 11 GPU training states. Frozen as full candidate 003; new CPU development validation launched (48 attempts, seed range starting 200, alternating palm models). This does not use the reserved held-out seed set.
  • Added feedback-learning branch: installed PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130, matching the package's allowed CPU version. Asymmetric-critic PPO actor gets six frames of q, qd, commanded-reference error and its own actions, initial commanded pose and time (504 numbers). Critic additionally gets simulated ball state/metrics; these are not actor inputs and will not be packaged. Actions are small joint-reference increments at 50 Hz, implemented through encoder feedback at 500 Hz and physical PD at 1 kHz. References freeze for the final 0.6 s; last 0.3 s evaluated strictly.
  • CUDA interoperation failed initially when capturing on an imported default Torch stream (unknown stream). Corrected by using a Warp-owned stream shared with Torch. Zero-action smoke test: all 22 worlds (11 physical catch states × two replicas) survived the full 3.8 s, average net yaw 0.522 degrees, zero task successes as expected. This verifies the held-grasp baseline before learning rotation. PPO training launched on GPU 2 with 1,056 worlds, 64-step rollouts, one CPU thread; logs include wall time, training action/physics throughput, GPU tensor memory and CPU seconds. [NVIDIA Warp/Torch interoperability documentation](https://nvidia.github.io/warp/latest/user_guide/interoperability/pytorch.html) consulted.
  • Saved a four-panel measured nominal trace figure (full_candidate_001_metrics.png) and nominal video. They are labelled as a local nominal pass that failed robustness. No target evaluation has been submitted.

  • Frozen full CPG candidate 003: nominal 1,466 degrees, 2.19 cm post-rest displacement and peak speed ratio 0.9776. There were 48 initialization attempts; invalid starts were reported and excluded, never counted as policy successes. The candidate was rejected for sealed submission.

New development seeds starting 300: 19/34 valid initial scenes passed the full task, catch 27/34, spin 27/34, hold 26/34 and hardware 26/34. This improvement remains far below credible transfer evidence. Reserved held-out seeds >=10000 remain unused.
  • Speed-governor diagnostic on eight representative development cases: original toss fraction 0.85/spin 0.70 gave 4/8 full passes and 6/8 hardware; toss 0.81/spin 0.60 or 0.65 gave 5/8 full and 8/8 hardware; toss 0.81/spin 0.70 gave 6/8 full and 7/8 hardware. Lower speed alone does not solve handoff failures.
  • Constructed a new broad training distribution, independently sampling radius 32.5–40.5 mm, mass 0.11–0.18 kg, sliding friction 0.4–1.2, torsion 0.0003–0.002, rolling friction 0.00001–0.00015, contact time 2.5–7 ms, damping 0.8–1.2, armature +/-20%, gains +/-5%, tilt +/-0.04 rad, ball inertia +/-20%, initial q noise 0.065 rad and both palm collision models. 96 physical initialization attempts (seeds 5000–5095) produced 59 valid rested starts. Failed prefixes remain represented in full-task validation and prepared-state toss training; they are explicitly excluded only from the isolated spin curriculum.
  • From-scratch PPO spin was stopped after about 200 updates / 13.6 million actor transitions: held the ball but mean signed rotation only about 89 degrees and no stage successes. Residual learning around the physically learned CPG was more effective. Narrow residual spin training on 11 known catches approached 99.8% stochastic stage success and ~1,750 degrees by update 350, but failed broad full-task checks. It was stopped at 22:37 and its GPU reallocated. These training numbers are not validation scores.
  • Broader residual spin v3 (GPU 2, 1,280 worlds) warm-started from the narrow actor at 22:24, added per-episode command jitter 0.004 rad and ball-position/velocity jitter 0.5 mm/0.002 m/s. By update 160 (~826 s) stage training success was 28.3%, mean 609 degrees. Most failures occur early during regrasp, consistent with markedly different settled finger configurations across physics. CPU/GPU scalar controller parity had already been verified; contact solver divergence remains independently challenged.
  • Added a slower 0.5 s regrasp and larger feedback authority (offset range tapering 0.4 to 0.2 rad over 0.8 s), warm-started with output rescaled to preserve late corrective size. Spin v4 launched on GPU 1 at 22:37; update 30 (~139 s) gave 40.1% stochastic training stage success and mean 803 degrees. This is a promising training trend, awaiting independent CPU checks.
  • Broad toss CMA v4 on GPU 3: 96 candidates x 58 physical prepared scenes, 84 independent joint/keyframe parameters, 0.81 speed cap, stronger brake gain 0.2, mean plus lower-quartile objective. At generation 201 (~1,437 s), best robust score 13.22; all-scenes training success has not been achieved. Separate PPO toss v1 launched on GPU 0 at 22:31 with 928 worlds; update 100 (~473 s) gave 59.9% stochastic toss/catch-stage success and average best-flight apex 5.87 cm. Marginal apex and missed catches remain concerns.
  • Timing-only study on the same 59 valid broad initial starts: (toss wait 1.3 s, spin duration 3.3 s) 9 full passes; (1.5,3.0) 12; (1.5,3.1) 8; (1.6,3.0) 10. Hardware 40–42/59, catches 35–36/59. Added waiting does not repair balls already lost during launch/regrasp. An initial analysis attempt errored because a lazy NumPy zip handle was inherited across multiprocessing; fixed by materializing arrays before forking and reran all reported timing results. No errored run was counted.
  • Static canonical-gait catch hypothesis rejected: ball dropped physically into the full candidate 004 gait's base pose across 58 broad physics settings was retained and rested in only 1/58. A fixed gait pose is not a credible universal landing configuration. Slower regrasp alone also failed: zero-residual broad-stage test 7.8% successful episodes, mean 303 degrees. Larger feedback authority is being evaluated rather than assuming the slower transition solves this.
  • Implemented deterministic NumPy-only launch-actor inference and composed it with the existing preparation and spin stages. New frozen snapshots now undergoing independent CPU stage and full tests; actor inputs remain encoder history, command/reference history, initial command and time, without object state.
  • Resource accounting: four L40S used concurrently for the four named research jobs; training logs include UTC, wall time, CPU seconds, actor/physics throughput and Torch allocated GPU memory. Broad spin training around 310k–365k physical world-steps/s; toss feedback about 255k/s. Warp model memory is additional to the explicitly labelled Torch allocation. Longer-history networks warm-start by mapping the existing six-frame weights to the newest six frames and assigning zero weights to older inputs; static initial-command/time features and privileged critic features retain their positions semantically.

Diagnosis: float32 actor max difference 1.56e-7, critic 2.10e-5; double-precision actor difference 1.11e-16 and critic 7.11e-15. Mapping is mathematically identical; the discrepancy is accumulation order from the larger matrix. Explicit evidence saved in history_extension_parity.json, rather than assuming the first assertion meant a feature-mapping defect.

  • Identified a concrete integration error in our GPU batching: MuJoCo Warp smooth._geom_local_to_global explicitly skips geoms welded to world, because their world frames are cached at make_data. Our per-model batched geom_pos/quat updates therefore did NOT update fixed palm collision world frames. All GPU worlds effectively used the FIRST model's fixed palm placement and mount tilt, even while other model fields differed. Earlier statements that GPU training exercised both palm geometries were incorrect. The initial parity check was too narrow (convex case); independent CPU validation results and CPU-generated state banks are unaffected.

2026-10-19 21:01–21:05 UTC — Demonstration and independent simulation (about 4 minutes)

  • Generated artifacts/demo_contact_sheet.jpg directly from video at 0.25 s intervals. Observed finger pushes, central support exchanges, and catches, with changes in which colored end points upward. Adopted alternating drive/support/catch phases; did not infer robot kinematics, pen mass, friction, or world-axis rotation from the human camera.
  • Implemented local_sim/model.py using supplied URDF transforms, masses, inertias, limits, mesh geometry, reflected armature, and documented PD gains. MuJoCo 3.16, 1 ms integration, force-limited actuators, free rigid pen, gravity, frictional contact. World mounting inferred as +90 degrees about URDF X so palm/flexion face upward. No target source used.
  • Found and fixed reference-pose error during construction: modifying MuJoCo qpos0 would redefine hinge reference geometry. Initial configuration is now set only on data.
  • Surveyed 48 combinations of MCP/PIP/resting placement (artifacts/start_survey.json/.jpg), 1.5 s settling each, 16 CPU workers; about 1.2 s simulation/survey compute. Selected estimated MCP=-0.2, PIP=1.2, DIP=0.3, abduction=0, matching cupped fingertips in screenshot. This is an assumption, not measured target encoder data. Pen length 15 cm, radius 4.5 mm, mass 18 g, friction 0.8 are nominal assumptions.
  • Dropping a pen from height caused placement transients; replaced with geometric lowering to first contact and gravity settling. Increased pre-episode settling to 8 s to match at-rest contract.
  • Hold-only baseline (12 s) retains contact, <0.2 cm travel, ~3 degrees tilt, <0.001 speed ratio but does not spin. Saved artifacts/hold_selfFalse.npz.
  • Collision audit initially enabling all self contacts produced persistent torque saturation from wrist/proximal-abduction housing hull overlap. Identified exact pairs at initial pose (up to 1.66 mm overlap). Excluded wrist/proximal-abduction housings in addition to directly adjacent links. This models intentional joint housing overlap; other finger/finger collisions remain enabled in subsequent searches. Must separately test simplification sensitivity.
  • First 5-knot smooth trajectory optimization: 180 CMA generations x64 candidates, 1.5 s per rollout, 24 CPU workers, 49.1 s. Best nominal trajectory rotates +66.85 degrees, max tilt 32.1 degrees, longest contact gap 9 ms, peak speed ratio .719, max drop .22 cm. Self collisions disabled in this initial search; it is not submission evidence. Saved artifacts/quarter_v1/best.npz, .mp4, .jpg. Continuing with corrected self collisions and at-rest initialization.
  • GPUs idle during CPU trajectory optimization. Installing CUDA PyTorch and MuJoCo Warp to investigate batched policy learning if trajectory search stalls. Official simulator references: https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/python.html and https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/mjwarp/index.html .

2026-10-19 21:09–21:13 UTC — GPU training operational and initial CPU transfer check

  • GPU probe compiled in 223 s, then simulated 256 worlds x1000 ticks in 0.581 s. Model uses same supplied robot meshes, inertias and actuators. No warning suppression used; candidate rollouts must be checked with CPU narrow-phase collision and resolution changes. 2026-10-19 21:15–21:18 UTC — Reward failure and grasp-handoff curriculum

  • Strong heading reward led to ejection: deterministic CPU rollout continues rotating in free fall, net +3557 degrees, but first contract violation at 2.192 s and only +115.58 degrees. It then has 8.84 s contact gap, >1 m excursion and hardware overspeed. All such rotations are invalid; no success claimed. Stopped this reward variant after roughly 25 million training samples and revised its failure penalty.
  • Blind seed 3 early deterministic CPU rollout: +76.19 degrees (+80.69 peak), max tilt 30.37 degrees, 6 ms gap, .277 speed ratio, final angular speed .248 rad/s; in-hand/hardware pass but spin fails. Mean inference .080 ms, max4.39 ms. artifacts/ppo_v2/cpu_early.*.
  • A handoff-stage hold test from the half-turn endpoint causes ring-finger back-drive overspeed (8.71x speed limit at 146 ms) and tilt violation. Short shooting horizons were hiding post-horizon instability. Full-horizon validation remains mandatory. This endpoint is rejected as a usable grasp.
  • New training curriculum samples only valid local rollout prefixes, with 50% resets still from the prescribed rest. Curriculum states may be moving; they are training roll-ins, not evaluation initial conditions. New blind PPO uses heading reward 10x per radian, the original survival/geometry penalties, and a 30-point failure cost. It must still pass complete at-rest CPU episodes and withheld conditions. GPU0 restarted with this revision.
  • Additional scoring correction: the short-horizon optimizer's smooth cost had omitted joint-position-limit excess (the exact validator already checks it). A current half-turn candidate first violates a position limit around 0.5 s despite an acceptable printed peak-speed ratio. Added explicit position-excess and longest-contact-gap penalties for future shooting runs. Only exact full metrics count as validity evidence; training progress summaries alone do not establish validity.

New gait reward penalizes travel only outside 5.5 cm and penalizes grasp failure by 50 points; adds joint-position-limit termination and soft penalty. Maintains zero force injection and freely moving pen.

  • Added a10x wider residual increment(.4 vs.04rad/action). A frozen old actor contributes clipped mean/10, with a zero-initialized learned correction. Initial deterministic increments match within3.73e-9rad on1024 teacher inputs and64 recurrent inputs; recurrent hidden states and inferred latents match bit-for-bit. The action range can now override the prior. All actual1ms hardware limits remain enforced.
  • Early expanded exploration changed the old clipped Gaussian distribution and caused short failures. Revised exploration/correction is almost zero before1s and ramps smoothly to full by1.5s, preserving the validated initial motion while exploring the handoff. GPU diagnosis of mean rollouts confirms source and expanded policies physically reach~400° before drop/tilt failure; outputs are captured before resets. Initial update0 means sample only early terminations and must not be interpreted as an unbiased first-episode rate.
  • The first recurrent expanded run had invalidly large PPO KL because updating its observer changed the ungated base mean at times with near-zero exploration variance. Discarded that run. The revised encoder_expanded_fixed freezes the original observer and base controller; only the correction head, action std and critic learn. This preserves the base path and gives a valid policy likelihood. It remains a research policy, not a validated candidate.

It keeps the first.6s physical prefix and1s terminal hold, so its search can also replace the forcing reference.

R2S2R-VIDEO-CUBE-2: EXPERIMENT_LOG.md (agent-written, 2026-10-20T03:25:12Z)

SuperDex cube research log

Scope and evidence rules

Start: 2026-10-19 20:59:00 UTC. Maximum research window: six hours (until 2026-10-20 02:59 UTC). Only supplied task files, demonstration, screenshot, vendor hardware and documented API are used. No target internals or prior experiments are accessed. Local model assumptions are not target measurements. No sealed submission without credible nominal and held-out validation.

2026-10-19 20:59–21:01 UTC — initial review (approximately 2 min)

  • Read README, TASK, SUCCESS, CONTRACT, SPECS, PACKAGE, REMOTE_API, demonstration provenance, example policy and initial URDF sections.
  • API budget: phase 1, 0/1 used; phase 2, 0/10 used. status: empty list. Connectivity verified before experiments. No submission made.
  • Hardware available: 48 logical CPU cores and four NVIDIA L40S (46 GB each), idle. System Python is 3.10.12; python is absent, python3 works. Initial research environment lacks numpy, scipy, torch and physics engines. The target Python 3.12 runtime differs; final policy must obey its dependency limits.
  • Reviewed target's two start views and demonstration frames at 1.1, 7.7, 14.3, 20.9 s. Human uses a thumb opposed to supporting fingers, isolates a half-cube for rotation and repeatedly repositions fingers/cube. Whole-cube reorientation supports access to different faces. These observations motivate contact-based turn/regrasp primitives; human joint trajectories and video timing are not directly transferable. Video is slowed by 0.5x and is not target calibration.
  • Target constraints: fixed wrist, 20 joints, 40 s at 500 Hz, 1 kHz torque-limited PD, no object sensing; arbitrary three-move U/R/F scramble known. Physical execution, not symbolic inversion, is expected to be the bottleneck.
  • Initial decision: construct a MuJoCo model from supplied inertias/meshes, independently model a passive legal cube mechanism, validate retention/contact/speed first, then optimize isolated layer primitives and compositions. Vary size, mass, friction, resistance and initial pose; reject model-assisted solutions and check mechanism/contact artifacts.

2026-10-19 21:01–21:05 UTC — simulator construction and grasp diagnosis (4 min)

  • Installed MuJoCo 3.16.0, numpy 2.2.6, scipy 1.15.3, trimesh 5.1.1, imageio and a CUDA PyTorch research environment. Research versions are distinct from the remote runtime.
  • Built an independent MJCF from the supplied URDF, preserving joint order, full inertias, limits, reflected armatures, torque-limited 1 kHz PD and mesh geometry. Mounted with URDF -Z toward world -X and URDF +Y toward world +Z, based on finger flexion geometry and screenshot.
  • Initial guessed grasp: cube dropped; peak speed ratio 8.99. Rejected as an intersecting initialization, not a policy result.
  • Geometry fitting iteration 1 retained a 50 mm, 60 g cube for 3 s, but it tilted substantially. Iteration 2 lowered/wrist-shifted the cube; after 10 s settling, cube centre (0.01143, 0.00123, 0.07480) m, maximum hand speed 1.91e-6 rad/s and small residual cube motion. Initialization transients are excluded from policy tests by beginning from the settled state. This start is an estimate, not a target measurement.
  • Inspection: settled cube rests on palm/proximal links, thumb loses contact. This is a meaningful grasp mismatch to investigate, not evidence of an effective turn.
  • Added passive, unactuated two-half hinge cube with resistance, viscous damping and periodic detent torque. It is ONLY a favorable isolated-axis primitive test, not a complete cube model; a three-axis interlocking mechanism is required before submission.
  • Built a compiled, multithreaded local shooting evaluator (research code only) to search smooth joint trajectories while retaining exact 1 kHz PD. It penalizes layer error, drop, hand speed, joint excess, lateral motion, and penetration. First CEM search: U clockwise, 192 candidates/iteration, 24 CPU threads, six keyframes over 4 s plus 0.7 s hold. No target evaluations used.
  • Technical references consulted: official MuJoCo XML and Python documentation at https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/XMLreference.html and https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/python.html (mesh/inertial conventions and equality constraint semantics).

2026-10-19 21:05–21:13 UTC — nominal primitive and adversarial model checks (8 min)

  • The U single-axis CEM finished 40 iterations in 456.39 s, simulating 34,909,261 1 kHz ticks with 24 CPU threads initially (later sharing CPUs with R/F searches). GPU training was not used: compiled CPU contact simulation is the current bottleneck. CUDA PyTorch 2.11.0+cu128 was verified to see all four L40S GPUs.
  • A six-second Python replay of an early U candidate: final angular error 5.22e-6 rad, max drop 1.975 mm, peak speed ratio 0.430, retained contact. Saved videos/u_v1.mp4, contact sheet and raw sampled trajectory. This is favorable single-axis simulation evidence only.
  • Preliminary 48-condition diagnostic: 44 valid placements; 12 passed (11/43 nonnominal). 20/44 met the final angle criterion, 14 exceeded joint speed, 4 dropped >5 cm. IMPORTANT protocol issue: this diagnostic read a changing optimizer checkpoint and is not a frozen-policy validation. It is retained only as failure diagnosis. Subsequent validation freezes/copies/hash-identifies the candidate and tightens placement rest/alignment checks.
  • Frozen earlier U snapshot SHA256: 79b2dcecfcc5e800a2ef6c1c58bb91b45effd0e5dcc4e52767a0005fde03932d. Three-axis mechanism challenge on this snapshot failed even the one-move U task: final maximum cubie orientation error 1.577 rad; zero completed turns; max speed ratio 0.192; drop 2.47 mm; retained contact. It loaded the F-axis seam first and jammed the intended U turn. This exposes the advantage conferred by the favorable isolated hinge.
  • Built a separate eight-corner surrogate: seven ball joints share the cube centre and a passive weld/connect network allows one seam at a time. Contact moments select the seam; aligned states permit reconnection. No desired move or solution is passed to the mechanism; no pose or velocity is assigned after initialization. Distributed static joint friction and internal opposite detent/damping moments provide resistance. This is still an uncalibrated mechanism approximation.
  • Physics checks: freefall COM drop over 0.1 s was 0.0485595 m versus 0.04905 m continuous prediction (one-step position reporting difference at 1 kHz). An extreme 0.015 N m internal torque fixture generated hundreds of rad/s: default equality impedance allowed ~9° deformation, so it was rejected. Increasing equality impedance reduced peak deformation to 1.11° and free-phase energy drift to ~0.11%; this stricter setting is used subsequently. Diagnostic fixture torques are never part of a manipulation policy.
  • An oriented IK grasp closer to the screenshot still settled down into the palm (z=0.0812 m rather than intended 0.106 m). This demonstrates the uncertainty in reconstructing a load-bearing start from images; initial encoder posture must be accounted for.
  • Launched independent R and F single-axis searches (128 candidates, 8 CPU threads each, 30 iterations). Added a compiled copy of the three-axis passive mechanism for faster policy search, pending agreement checks against Python.
  • Frozen final U candidate for independent 64-condition validation; predeclared seed 6030412; 6 CPU workers. Randomized size 45–55 mm, mass 40–90 g, friction 0.4–1.2, resistance 3–20 mN m, detent 2–12 mN m, initial rotation ±15°, translation and joint targets. These are research assumptions, not measured target bounds.

2026-10-19 21:13–21:22 UTC — competing-axis learning and collision-model audit (9 min)

  • Final isolated U frozen validation (SHA256 1aa09a33e0a8102c4320cb1d618d298f948f4c8bb1b27243fbee430678719fda): 64 samples, 44 valid at-rest aligned placements, 18 passed total, 17/43 held-out passed. A placement failure is reported separately and is not counted as a manipulation success.
  • Symbolic sign audit: all nine U/R/F face/power moves composed with their inverse return all cubie orientations to identity. The earlier full-mechanism U check used a clockwise primitive against U rather than U' and was not the matching inverse test. Its observed zero-turn behavior still established a physical failure; no solve-rate claim is inferred from that mismatched test. Corrected full-U learning uses scramble U' and learns U clockwise. The initial, mismatched warm-start run was interrupted and retained separately.
  • Compiled/Python full-model agreement on the same six-second failure: max cubie errors 1.576263 and 1.576199 rad, peak speed ratios 0.19142 and 0.19198, drops 2.473 and 2.452 mm. Native command interpolation was subsequently corrected to exactly 500 Hz held for two 1 kHz ticks; added an optional encoder-based compliant controller and final-state export. Research extensions are separate from policy packaging.
  • Correct competing-axis U CEM found a nominal one-move solve on iteration 0; later candidates improved to max orientation error 0.00433 rad, speed ratio 0.214, drop 1.53 mm, one completed turn, mechanism deviation 0.0691 rad. This establishes nominal one-move feasibility in this surrogate, not full-task success.
  • Frozen full-U development validation (SHA256 0d79b717fc3e500f6e492c2a0bf0f1a5e988f995451ed94f20adedd28897d335): 32 samples, 30 valid placements, 15 passed total, 14/29 held-out passed over 12 s. It remains far below justified transfer reliability. Initial placement uses the aligned passive constraint network as an operator fixture; it is released before the first policy tick, with no object pose/velocity resets during rollout. Per-scenario states and parameters are saved for replay.
  • Investigated convex collision assumptions using exact triangle ray intersections on the supplied palm. At 12 sampled points, the single convex hull places the upper surface 0.034–5.686 mm above the actual mesh; its volume is 1.34 times the mesh volume. This can artificially support the cube and invalidates any claim of robustness to contact geometry. Began a 1 mm CoACD decomposition of the supplied wrist mesh; original vendor inertia and visual mesh will remain intact. Source: https://github.com/SarahWeiii/CoACD.
  • Full-model cyclic/canonical R and F searches started: each returns joints to the initial target and penalizes residual cube-axis misalignment, to test composability rather than a single isolated turn. Stopped noncyclic U search after ~19 iterations to redirect its 16 CPU threads to a cyclic/canonical U primitive. Simple hand-designed abduction sweeps failed to turn U and were rejected.
  • Demonstration contact sheet saved. Full 12-frame review confirms acquisition, alternating support and turn, significant whole-cube reorientation, and final placement onto a surface. Only manipulation/contact organization is adopted; acquisition, wrist transport, and final table placement are excluded by this task.

2026-10-19 21:22–21:35 UTC — full-task composition failure and revised learning (13 min)

  • Implemented a deterministic policy that reads the supplied scramble and initial target encoders, selects precomputed cube plans, and executes calibrated smooth joint macros. Whole-cube reorientation from each local macro is included in the symbolic transition model. Offline reverse search covered all 729 possible three-row inputs (385 unique orientation-quotiented states) with at most nine four-second macros. Independent algebra identifies 324 input sequences whose states are exactly three half-turn-metric moves from solved. This is symbolic coverage, not physical success.
  • First frozen macro bank, SHA256 e87a82009893170f20091a50f9943b1dc6c81c2e7fd2b96eaae06ba5ded31c65: 0/12 full-task nominal scrambles passed; 0/12 passed using the decomposed palm. All placements were valid. Typical failures: accumulated cube pose error, unintended seam selection/jam, then drop. Local evaluation stops after an irreversible >7 cm drop to save compute; successful candidates run the full 40 s. No such early stopping is used in the saved 40 s Python video.
  • Saved videos/full_u_nominal.mp4 (12 s one-move success), videos/full_u_heldout_failure.mp4 (12 s missed-turn failure), and videos/bank_v1_three_move_failure.mp4 (full 40 s task failure).
