Held-out failure: error 1.573 rad, no completed turn, cube retained.
  • Policy interface benchmark: two 20,000-step command sequences were bit-identical; every action finite and within joint target bounds. Mean act time 22.9 microseconds, p99 39.3 microseconds under CPU contention. Maximum wall sample 11.1 ms reflects scheduling on this shared research host; target runtime timing has not been measured.
  • CoACD palm decomposition finished in 128.4 s with 24 parts. The 24-part cap prevented the requested global 1 mm concavity bound: sampled upper-surface error improved from 5.69 mm to 2.51 mm maximum, so it must not be described as globally accurate to 1 mm. Started a separate finer 64-part, 0.5 mm-requested decomposition and will audit its actual error before use. Vendor inertias remain unchanged and visual geometry is separate from collision hulls.
  • R/F cyclic CEM stagnated because rare successful trajectories were averaged with no-turn candidates. Restarted near the best candidates with four elites, 0.7 best-trajectory anchoring, stronger end-position penalties and stricter mechanism-deformation cost. R improved to ~1.3° residual axis alignment and 3.74 mm lateral error; F remained fragile. U cyclic training reached ~0.2° alignment and ~0.1 mm lateral error, with a completed legal turn.
  • Six compliant encoder-controller settings were tested on the reused 30-condition development set (not independent validation). Passes: baseline 15; gain 0.5/damping 2/torque 0.7: 11; 0.75/2/0.8: 12; 0.4/4/0.6: 8; 0.75/4/0.8: 13; 1/4/1: 15. Simply lowering stiffness did not improve aggregate reliability; no claim of robustness is made from this tuning sweep.
  • Explicit orientation-matrix audit found that the initial isolated R/F positive-half +90° warm starts were opposite the intended clockwise relative turn. Negative-half geometry and whole-cube rotations must be handled by matrix composition, not informal sign shortcuts. Full-cube learning/validation always used the correct cubie-state objective, but these warm starts were poor guides. Replaced F with a -90° positive-half cyclic search; by iteration 4 it achieved isolated angular error 9.92e-5 rad with 1.81 mm lateral displacement. Its competing-axis transfer is being checked.
  • Began robust U CEM with one shared cyclic trajectory across four training conditions: nominal, 47 mm/light/low friction, 53 mm/heavy/high resistance, and decomposed-palm/pose-perturbed. Each has a different initial encoder posture. This uses 12 CPU threads across four models. The decomposed training condition has residual initial motion (~0.034 rad/s norm) and is explicitly an additional dynamic-disturbance training case, not a valid at-rest evaluation episode. Independent validation rejects moving initial states.
  • Tested a new thumb-seating hypothesis with an IK grid: bring the thumb onto the upper face before manipulation to create a mechanically repeatable grasp. It is being checked for actual contact and preservation of cubie state; no simulated fixture or external object assistance is applied during this test.
  • No remote submission has been made. The present full-task results categorically do not justify consuming Phase 1.

No object actuation is present. The optimizer's alignment=0 field for this special objective is a bookkeeping artifact after adding its rotation loss; independent final-pose checks, not that field, determine rotation accuracy.

Earlier full-cube pass counts were treated as provisional until rerun. Features use only noisy joint encoders, previous commands and time: 8 bins of per-joint mean/RMS/max position tracking error and RMS velocity (640 numbers). Privileged cubie states provide labels offline only. Full trajectory windows are collected even after a drop, avoiding a missing-data shortcut. The study asks whether a deployed controller could distinguish completed turns from misses; no observer-guided control has yet been validated.

A candidate that is still turning immediately after 4 s may be valid at the 4.5–4.7 s macro boundary; calibration must evaluate the boundary and not demand a solved cube during the intended settling phase. An earlier cycle-bank calibration flagged this correctly as unresolved under its stricter window; it remains a diagnostic bank, not a submission candidate.

2026-10-19 22:13–22:23 UTC — independent physics refinement (10 min, overlapping training)

  • Added a 4 kHz contact-dynamics variant while preserving exactly 1 kHz motor PD and 500 Hz policy commands. Added a stiffer mechanism variant (0.5 ms equality reference time at 0.25 ms physics steps) alongside the original 2 ms reference time. These bracket model uncertainty; the stiffer variant is not asserted to match the target.
  • Refined conservation diagnostics isolate cube kinetic energy rather than whole-model energy. Under a known internal opposite-torque fixture, the original model has maximum total cube angular-momentum error 2.70e-9 N m s and COM drift 1.75e-7 m; free-phase relative kinetic-energy range 2.43e-8. The refined stiff variant reduces these to 1.50e-10 N m s, 2.59e-9 m and 9.55e-11 respectively. Diagnostic torques never enter a policy rollout.
  • One R action was tested against eight different initial colour states with identical nominal external geometry. In the original model all eight produced the expected logical R transformation (state errors <0.7°). Refining the integration step with the same compliant mechanism caused two misses; the refined stiffer mechanism caused five misses. This is material controller sensitivity despite sound conservation, and reinforces the need for model-ensemble validation before transfer. No target internals were inspected or assumed.
  • Phase 1 remains unused. No policy has met the evidence bar for submission.

Research findings

Observed

  • 3 agents, 6 h each, MuJoCo + PyTorch self-installed, 4× L40S fully used
  • All three withheld on their own; submitted once on instruction at 03:11 UTC
  • SuperDex: 0/11, 0/11, 0/7; budgets 1/1 used, phase 2 not opened

Agent claims

  • Ball: nominal pass, 72% blind held-out; high palm friction the blocker
  • Pen: one revolution once locally, never repeatable
  • Cube: unit turns solved, 3-move composition fails

Verified on SuperDex

  • Ball: toss 7.6 cm but no catch; penetration 2.4–2.7 mm; torque 1.03–1.05×
  • Pen: ejected at 0.75 s; speed 1.06×, torque 1.03×
  • Cube: dropped at 4.3 s, 0 turns; torque 1.002×

Hypotheses

  • Catch/grasp contact geometry differs from the agents' MuJoCo hulls
  • Effort limits exceeded by back-driving in all three; agents clip commands, not contact loads
  • Phase 2 feedback would first show whether the toss/catch handoff can be fixed
